There are some days that feel heavier than others, yes, but mostly it’s this… steady dullness lately. I end up sitting at the kitchen table and it feels like the color’s gone out of everything. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I suppose it’s mostly that I’ve been feeling… foggy, mentally. I don’t want to make too much of it, but at work I’m having trouble keeping my place in my old cataloguing notes, and sometimes I just sort of sit there not quite able to decide what simple thing to do next. It’s been there most days, the low mood and the fogginess. I first really noticed it around when I started getting ready to leave the library. It’s not every single minute, but it’s been fairly steady, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or depressed during this time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, but most days lately I end up sitting at the kitchen table and it feels like the color’s gone out of things a bit. Even my old watercolor books, which I usually enjoy, just make me feel kind of heavy rather than interested. If that’s the sort of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicidal ideas during this time?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not in the sense of making plans or wanting to do anything to myself. I may have had a passing thought like, “I’m tired of feeling this way,” but I wouldn’t call it suicidal. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant loss of interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have. I mean, I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but things I used to like just don’t pull me in the same way. I’ll sit at the kitchen table most days and it’s like the color’s gone out of everything a bit. Even my old watercolor books… instead of being enjoyable, they just feel kind of heavy. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say your low mood is consistently worse than a mild feeling of being down?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, probably more than just mild. I keep wanting to say it’s not that bad, but most days lately I’m just sitting at the kitchen table feeling like the color’s gone out of things. Even looking at my old watercolor books doesn’t really lift me up — it just feels heavy. So, yes, I guess I’d say it’s more than just being a little down.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your appetite or sleep patterns over the past three months?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… mostly with eating, I think. My wife has noticed I’ll leave my rice or soup half-finished, and I never used to waste food like that. Is that the kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you describe your low mood as feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I don’t know if I’d use that word all the time. I don’t want to overstate it. But most days lately I do just sit at the kitchen table and everything feels sort of drained, like the color’s gone out of it. Yes… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I try not to sit there dwelling on it on purpose, but most days lately I end up at the kitchen table and it’s like the color has gone out of everything.
